========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi. Um, well, I'm here because I just... I keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's like a switch flips. Like, I work retail, right? And the other day I completely yelled at one of my employees for putting stock on the wrong shelf. Which, honestly, the shelving units in our store are a nightmare anyway. Corporate sent down these new metal racks last month and they don't even fit the endcaps properly, so the boxes are always sliding around. I told my district manager about it, but he never listens, he just sends more emails about our numbers. 

But anyway, I just screamed at this poor kid over the stock, and afterward I had to go sit in the back room because I was just shaking. Literally shaking. Because that is not how I usually treat people. I'm usually very patient with my team. 

The traffic getting to your office was awful, by the way. They have Main Street completely torn up for that water main thing, and the detour signs make no sense. I almost missed the turn into the parking lot. But yeah, the yelling... I just can't keep snapping at people like that.

Um, other episodes... well, yeah, I guess. It's been happening a lot lately. Just, like, zero to sixty, you know? Like last week, I was at the grocery store, just trying to get some dinner after a double shift. And the cashier was moving so slow. I mean, painfully slow. And they were bagging my bread with the canned goods! Who does that? I used to bag groceries when I was in high school, back at this little independent market that isn't even there anymore—they tore it down to build that new apartment complex, the one with the weird modern balconies? They look like they're going to fall off, honestly. I don't know who approved that design. 

But anyway, I just... I snapped at her. I told her she was going to ruin my groceries and I grabbed the bags and practically ran out of the store. I was so embarrassed. But I just couldn't stop myself. It's like I'm just so on edge all the time now. I don't know exactly how often it's happening, but it feels like it's all the time. My husband even said something about it yesterday when I got mad about the dishwasher not being loaded right. He always puts the bowls on the bottom rack, and they never get clean that way. I've told him a million times. It's just basic physics, the water needs to reach the inside... but yeah, I just exploded at him. It's bad. It's really bad.

I told my district manager about it, but he never listens, he just sends more emails about our numbers. \n\nBut anyway, I just screamed at this poor kid over the stock, and afterward I had to go sit in the back room because I was just shaking. Am I angry all the time? Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. It's this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. 

And the neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's sweet but she always leaves her recycling bins out for like three or four days after pickup, and the wind blows the plastic bottles all over the street. The HOA sent out a letter about it last month, but they print everything on this really cheap, flimsy paper that just gets stuck in the mailbox hinges. 

But anyway, I wasn't mad about any of that yesterday. I was just enjoying the morning and watering the plants. So, no, I don't feel irritable constantly... it just sort of bubbles up and explodes out of nowhere when those specific moments happen.

Guilty? Oh, God, yes. It's awful. Like I said, I'm really here because I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's so out of character for me. Like last week, I yelled at one of my new employees for putting the backstock on the wrong shelf in the stockroom. I mean, I really let her have it. And afterward, I went into the breakroom and I was literally shaking, just trembling all over, because that is not how I usually treat people. I've always prided myself on being a good manager. 

When I first started in retail, my manager was this woman named Brenda, and she was a nightmare. She used to make us clock out and then stay to sweep the floors, which I'm pretty sure is highly illegal. I tried to tell my district manager about it, but he was always at these corporate retreats in Florida playing golf. They always go to Florida, even in the middle of summer when it's just a swamp. My sister went to Disney World in July once, and she said her kids were just melting on the pavement. They spent half their vacation money on those little battery-operated fan misters that break after two days. 

But anyway, yeah... I feel terrible after I snap. Just so ashamed. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.

Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. It's this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. \n\nAnd the neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's sweet but she always leaves her recycling bins out for like three or four days after pickup, and the wind blows the plastic bottles all over the street. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Serious consequences? Um, yeah. Yeah, it's definitely causing problems. I mean, I've completely missed a vendor meeting because I was just too overwhelmed, and the other day I literally just walked out before my shift was even over. I just couldn't do it. And on top of that, I can't even bring myself to go back to the house to deal with the damage, so my work stuff and my home responsibilities are just piling up way faster than I can handle. 

Which is stressing my husband out, too. He’s been trying to deal with the insurance adjuster, but those guys are impossible to get on the phone. They always put you on hold with that awful elevator music. I don't understand why companies can't just play normal music, or at least a podcast or something while you wait. My nephew started a podcast last year—something about retro video games. He bought all this expensive microphone equipment and set up a whole studio in his parents' basement. My sister was furious because he took down all her old sewing tables to make room for it. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. She made one for my cousin's baby shower, but my cousin ended up moving to Arizona, and who needs a heavy quilt in Arizona? It’s like 110 degrees there in the shade. 

But, um, yeah... my responsibilities are just... they're completely piling up. Work and home. It's getting really bad.

I tried to tell my district manager about it, but he was always at these corporate retreats in Florida playing golf. They always go to Florida, even in the middle of summer when it's just a swamp. My sister went to Disney World in July once, and she said her kids were just melting on the pavement. They spent half their vacation money on those little battery-operated fan misters that break after two days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I feel terrible after I snap. Just so ashamed. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. They have the right lane completely blocked off with those orange barrels, and they've been sitting there for three weeks and I haven't seen a single worker actually doing anything. They just leave the heavy machinery parked on the grass median. \n\nMy husband says it's because the city ran out of funding for the project halfway through, which wouldn't surprise me at all. Our property taxes went up again this year, and for what? Potholes? We have this one pothole at the entrance of our neighborhood that is so deep, I swear it's going to rip the axle right off my Honda. I drive a CR-V, which I usually love, but the newer models have this weird touchscreen thing for the radio that is so distracting when you're trying to drive. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road. Now you have to tap this little screen three times just to turn down the air conditioning. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no, my emotions are fine otherwise. It's just... it's just those sudden moments where I just lose it. And on top of that, I can't even bring myself to go back to the house to deal with the damage, so my work stuff and my home responsibilities are just piling up way faster than I can handle. \n\nWhich is stressing my husband out, too. Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word.

My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, bought one of those "squirrel-proof" bird feeders—the ones that spin around when something heavy lands on them. He paid like eighty dollars for it at that fancy wild bird store downtown. The store is right next to this little Italian bakery that makes the most incredible cannolis. They fill them right when you order so the shell doesn't get soggy. I brought a box to work once for a birthday, and they were gone in five minutes. 

But anyway, no, I... I remember the outbursts perfectly. I'm definitely in control of my actions, I'm just... I'm just really angry in the moment.

I tried their iced caramel thing once and it was way too sweet. Like drinking straight syrup. I don't know how people drink that every morning.\n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, when I snap at people. No, I don't really notice my heart racing or sweating right then. Just that shaking thing I told you about afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find yourself feeling consistently irritable or angry most of the time?\n\nPatient: Am I angry all the time? Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. It's this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. \n\nAnd the neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's sweet but she always leaves her recycling bins out for like three or four days after pickup, and the wind blows the plastic bottles all over the street. The HOA sent out a letter about it last month, but they print everything on this really cheap, flimsy paper that just gets stuck in the mailbox hinges. \n\nBut anyway, I wasn't mad about any of that yesterday. I was just enjoying the morning and watering the plants. So, no, I don't feel irritable constantly... it just sort of bubbles up and explodes out of nowhere when those specific moments happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens in the time between these outbursts? Do you ever feel guilty, ashamed, or regretful after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, God, yes. It's awful. Like I said, I'm really here because I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's so out of character for me. Like last week, I yelled at one of my new employees for putting the backstock on the wrong shelf in the stockroom. I mean, I really let her have it. And afterward, I went into the breakroom and I was literally shaking, just trembling all over, because that is not how I usually treat people. I've always prided myself on being a good manager. \n\nWhen I first started in retail, my manager was this woman named Brenda, and she was a nightmare. She used to make us clock out and then stay to sweep the floors, which I'm pretty sure is highly illegal. I tried to tell my district manager about it, but he was always at these corporate retreats in Florida playing golf. They always go to Florida, even in the middle of summer when it's just a swamp. My sister went to Disney World in July once, and she said her kids were just melting on the pavement. They spent half their vacation money on those little battery-operated fan misters that break after two days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I feel terrible after I snap. Just so ashamed. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. They have the right lane completely blocked off with those orange barrels, and they've been sitting there for three weeks and I haven't seen a single worker actually doing anything. They just leave the heavy machinery parked on the grass median. \n\nMy husband says it's because the city ran out of funding for the project halfway through, which wouldn't surprise me at all. Our property taxes went up again this year, and for what? Potholes? We have this one pothole at the entrance of our neighborhood that is so deep, I swear it's going to rip the axle right off my Honda. I drive a CR-V, which I usually love, but the newer models have this weird touchscreen thing for the radio that is so distracting when you're trying to drive. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road. Now you have to tap this little screen three times just to turn down the air conditioning. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no, my emotions are fine otherwise. It's just... it's just those sudden moments where I just lose it. The rest of the time I feel pretty normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have any of these outbursts led to serious consequences, like problems at work, relationship issues, or legal trouble?\n\nPatient: Serious consequences? Um, yeah. Yeah, it's definitely causing problems. I mean, I've completely missed a vendor meeting because I was just too overwhelmed, and the other day I literally just walked out before my shift was even over. I just couldn't do it. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. She made one for my cousin's baby shower, but my cousin ended up moving to Arizona, and who needs a heavy quilt in Arizona? It’s like 110 degrees there in the shade. \n\nBut, um, yeah... my responsibilities are just... they're completely piling up. Work and home. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: During these outbursts, do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions or have trouble remembering what happened while you were angry?\n\nPatient: Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word. \n\nSpeaking of remembering things, my memory has been terrible lately with groceries. Persistently angry? No... no, I wouldn't say that. Once I calm down from one of those moments, I'm just kind of back to normal. I'm not walking around irritable all day. I actually try to keep things pretty peaceful.

I don't know, I drink a lot of coffee, so I guess I'm always a little wired anyway. There’s this new drive-thru coffee place that just opened up right next to our plaza. The line is always spilling out onto the main road, which makes getting into our parking lot a total nightmare. People will literally just sit there blocking traffic for a seven-dollar latte. I tried their iced caramel thing once and it was way too sweet. Like drinking straight syrup. I don't know how people drink that every morning.\n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, when I snap at people. No, I don't really notice my heart racing or sweating right then. Just that shaking thing I told you about afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find yourself feeling consistently irritable or angry most of the time?\n\nPatient: Am I angry all the time? Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. It's this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. \n\nAnd the neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's sweet but she always leaves her recycling bins out for like three or four days after pickup, and the wind blows the plastic bottles all over the street. The HOA sent out a letter about it last month, but they print everything on this really cheap, flimsy paper that just gets stuck in the mailbox hinges. \n\nBut anyway, I wasn't mad about any of that yesterday. I was just enjoying the morning and watering the plants. So, no, I don't feel irritable constantly... it just sort of bubbles up and explodes out of nowhere when those specific moments happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens in the time between these outbursts? Do you ever feel guilty, ashamed, or regretful after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, God, yes. It's awful. Like I said, I'm really here because I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's so out of character for me. Like last week, I yelled at one of my new employees for putting the backstock on the wrong shelf in the stockroom. I mean, I really let her have it. And afterward, I went into the breakroom and I was literally shaking, just trembling all over, because that is not how I usually treat people. I've always prided myself on being a good manager. \n\nWhen I first started in retail, my manager was this woman named Brenda, and she was a nightmare. She used to make us clock out and then stay to sweep the floors, which I'm pretty sure is highly illegal. I tried to tell my district manager about it, but he was always at these corporate retreats in Florida playing golf. They always go to Florida, even in the middle of summer when it's just a swamp. My sister went to Disney World in July once, and she said her kids were just melting on the pavement. They spent half their vacation money on those little battery-operated fan misters that break after two days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I feel terrible after I snap. Just so ashamed. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. They have the right lane completely blocked off with those orange barrels, and they've been sitting there for three weeks and I haven't seen a single worker actually doing anything. They just leave the heavy machinery parked on the grass median. \n\nMy husband says it's because the city ran out of funding for the project halfway through, which wouldn't surprise me at all. Our property taxes went up again this year, and for what? Potholes? We have this one pothole at the entrance of our neighborhood that is so deep, I swear it's going to rip the axle right off my Honda. I drive a CR-V, which I usually love, but the newer models have this weird touchscreen thing for the radio that is so distracting when you're trying to drive. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road. Now you have to tap this little screen three times just to turn down the air conditioning. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no, my emotions are fine otherwise. It's just... it's just those sudden moments where I just lose it. Um, yeah. Yeah, it's definitely causing problems. He bought all this expensive microphone equipment and set up a whole studio in his parents' basement. My sister was furious because he took down all her old sewing tables to make room for it. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. She made one for my cousin's baby shower, but my cousin ended up moving to Arizona, and who needs a heavy quilt in Arizona? It’s like 110 degrees there in the shade. \n\nBut, um, yeah... my responsibilities are just... they're completely piling up. Work and home. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: During these outbursts, do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions or have trouble remembering what happened while you were angry?\n\nPatient: Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word. \n\nSpeaking of remembering things, my memory has been terrible lately with groceries. I went to the store yesterday specifically for coffee filters and milk, and I walked out with two bags of potting soil and a new garden hose. I don't even have a garden anymore. We used to have this beautiful little raised bed in the backyard where I grew cherry tomatoes, but the squirrels kept getting to them before they were ripe. Squirrels are so smart, you know? My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" bird feeders—the ones that spin around when something heavy lands on them. He paid like eighty dollars for it at that fancy wild bird store downtown. The store is right next to this little Italian bakery that makes the most incredible cannolis. I remember the outbursts perfectly. I'm definitely in control of my actions, I'm just... I'm just really angry in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever find yourself feeling persistently angry or irritable, even if you’re not lashing out?\n\nPatient: Persistently angry? Unusually sad or sensitive? Um, no, not really. I don't think little things are getting to me more than usual. I'm not, like, crying at commercials or anything. Though, honestly, I haven't even watched much TV lately because the remote at the temporary place we're staying is completely broken. The buttons just stick. I've told him a million times. It's just basic physics, the water needs to reach the inside... but yeah, I just exploded at him. It's bad. It's really bad.\n\nDoctor: When you feel yourself getting angry like that, do you notice any other physical symptoms — like a racing heart, sweating, or feeling really restless?\n\nPatient: Physical symptoms? Like sweating or my heart racing? Um, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I do sweat at work, but that’s just because our store’s air conditioning has been broken since May. May! Can you believe that? Corporate refuses to send someone out to fix it. They keep telling me they put in a work order with this local HVAC company, but I actually know the owner of that company—his daughter used to take dance classes with my niece—and he said they never even got a call from our corporate office. It's just ridiculous. We have these tiny little plastic desk fans at the registers, which do absolutely nothing when it's ninety degrees out. \n\nAnd as for feeling restless... I don't know, I drink a lot of coffee, so I guess I'm always a little wired anyway. There’s this new drive-thru coffee place that just opened up right next to our plaza. The line is always spilling out onto the main road, which makes getting into our parking lot a total nightmare. People will literally just sit there blocking traffic for a seven-dollar latte. I tried their iced caramel thing once and it was way too sweet. Like drinking straight syrup. I don't know how people drink that every morning.\n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, when I snap at people. No, I don't really notice my heart racing or sweating right then. Just that shaking thing I told you about afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find yourself feeling consistently irritable or angry most of the time?\n\nPatient: Am I angry all the time? Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. It's this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. \n\nAnd the neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's sweet but she always leaves her recycling bins out for like three or four days after pickup, and the wind blows the plastic bottles all over the street. The HOA sent out a letter about it last month, but they print everything on this really cheap, flimsy paper that just gets stuck in the mailbox hinges. \n\nBut anyway, I wasn't mad about any of that yesterday. I was just enjoying the morning and watering the plants. So, no, I don't feel irritable constantly... it just sort of bubbles up and explodes out of nowhere when those specific moments happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens in the time between these outbursts? Do you ever feel guilty, ashamed, or regretful after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, God, yes. It's awful. Like I said, I'm really here because I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's so out of character for me. Like last week, I yelled at one of my new employees for putting the backstock on the wrong shelf in the stockroom. I mean, I really let her have it. And afterward, I went into the breakroom and I was literally shaking, just trembling all over, because that is not how I usually treat people. I've always prided myself on being a good manager. \n\nWhen I first started in retail, my manager was this woman named Brenda, and she was a nightmare. She used to make us clock out and then stay to sweep the floors, which I'm pretty sure is highly illegal. I tried to tell my district manager about it, but he was always at these corporate retreats in Florida playing golf. They always go to Florida, even in the middle of summer when it's just a swamp. My sister went to Disney World in July once, and she said her kids were just melting on the pavement. They spent half their vacation money on those little battery-operated fan misters that break after two days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I feel terrible after I snap. Just so ashamed. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. They have the right lane completely blocked off with those orange barrels, and they've been sitting there for three weeks and I haven't seen a single worker actually doing anything. They just leave the heavy machinery parked on the grass median. \n\nMy husband says it's because the city ran out of funding for the project halfway through, which wouldn't surprise me at all. Our property taxes went up again this year, and for what? Potholes? We have this one pothole at the entrance of our neighborhood that is so deep, I swear it's going to rip the axle right off my Honda. I drive a CR-V, which I usually love, but the newer models have this weird touchscreen thing for the radio that is so distracting when you're trying to drive. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road. Now you have to tap this little screen three times just to turn down the air conditioning. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no, my emotions are fine otherwise. It's just... it's just those sudden moments where I just lose it. The rest of the time I feel pretty normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have any of these outbursts led to serious consequences, like problems at work, relationship issues, or legal trouble?\n\nPatient: Serious consequences? Um, yeah. Yeah, it's definitely causing problems. I mean, I've completely missed a vendor meeting because I was just too overwhelmed, and the other day I literally just walked out before my shift was even over. I just couldn't do it. And on top of that, I can't even bring myself to go back to the house to deal with the damage, so my work stuff and my home responsibilities are just piling up way faster than I can handle. \n\nWhich is stressing my husband out, too. He’s been trying to deal with the insurance adjuster, but those guys are impossible to get on the phone. They always put you on hold with that awful elevator music. I don't understand why companies can't just play normal music, or at least a podcast or something while you wait. My nephew started a podcast last year—something about retro video games. He bought all this expensive microphone equipment and set up a whole studio in his parents' basement. My sister was furious because he took down all her old sewing tables to make room for it. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. She made one for my cousin's baby shower, but my cousin ended up moving to Arizona, and who needs a heavy quilt in Arizona? It’s like 110 degrees there in the shade. \n\nBut, um, yeah... my responsibilities are just... they're completely piling up. Work and home. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: During these outbursts, do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions or have trouble remembering what happened while you were angry?\n\nPatient: Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word. \n\nSpeaking of remembering things, my memory has been terrible lately with groceries. I went to the store yesterday specifically for coffee filters and milk, and I walked out with two bags of potting soil and a new garden hose. I don't even have a garden anymore. We used to have this beautiful little raised bed in the backyard where I grew cherry tomatoes, but the squirrels kept getting to them before they were ripe. Squirrels are so smart, you know? My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" bird feeders—the ones that spin around when something heavy lands on them. He paid like eighty dollars for it at that fancy wild bird store downtown. The store is right next to this little Italian bakery that makes the most incredible cannolis. They fill them right when you order so the shell doesn't get soggy. I brought a box to work once for a birthday, and they were gone in five minutes. \n\nBut anyway, no, I... I remember the outbursts perfectly. I'm definitely in control of my actions, I'm just... I'm just really angry in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever find yourself feeling persistently angry or irritable, even if you’re not lashing out?\n\nPatient: Persistently angry? No... no, I wouldn't say that. Once I calm down from one of those moments, I'm just kind of back to normal. I'm not walking around irritable all day. I actually try to keep things pretty peaceful. \n\nI even started drinking chamomile tea in the evenings to just, you know, relax. Have you ever tried the loose-leaf kind? My coworker Brenda swears by it. She buys it from this little holistic shop a few towns over. Honestly, it smells a bit like dried grass to me, but Brenda is super into all that natural wellness stuff lately. She even started making her own soap out of goat's milk. She bought two actual pygmy goats last spring to keep in her backyard! They are adorable, but they literally eat everything in sight. They chewed right through her husband's favorite pair of gardening gloves that he left on the patio. He was furious because they were those expensive, thick leather ones you have to special order online. He uses them for his rose bushes. He grows those really big heirloom roses, the ones that smell amazing but have thorns like daggers. I tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles absolutely decimated them in like three days. They just turn the leaves into lace, it's awful. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, being irritable all the time. No, I don't just sit around feeling angry. It's really just those sudden blow-ups, and then I'm fine. Well, not fine, but not angry.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever feel unusually sad, anxious, or emotionally sensitive — like small things affect your mood more than they used to?\n\nPatient: Unusually sad or sensitive? Um, no, not really. I don't think little things are getting to me more than usual. I'm not, like, crying at commercials or anything. Though, honestly, I haven't even watched much TV lately because the remote at the temporary place we're staying is completely broken. The buttons just stick. It's one of those cheap universal remotes, which I always find so confusing anyway. \n\nMy dad used to have this massive universal remote in the nineties, it was practically the size of a textbook. He was so proud of it because it could control the VCR and the stereo at the exact same time. He used to listen to all these old jazz cassettes on that stereo. Mostly Miles Davis and John Coltrane. Drained? Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but that's mostly just from work. Standing behind a register for eight hours really does a number on your arches, you know? I actually had to buy these special orthopedic inserts last week. They cost like forty dollars at the pharmacy down the street.

Anyway... wait, I'm doing it again. What did you ask me? Oh, right, feeling down or drained after I get mad. No, I don't really get depressed or overly sensitive afterward. I just kind of want to move past it and get back to whatever I was doing.

Trouble functioning? Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I actually missed a vendor meeting last week, which is really unlike me. And a couple of days ago, I just walked out before my shift was even over. I couldn't take being in the store anymore. Plus, I just... I can't face going back to the damaged house right now to deal with any of it, so all my home stuff and my work responsibilities are just piling up faster than I can handle them. 

My manager at work, Gary, he was pretty upset when I walked out. He’s one of those guys who takes retail very, very seriously, you know? Like, he wears a tie every single day, even though it’s just a hardware store and half the time we’re covered in dust from the lumber aisle. He actually spilled coffee down his tie the other morning and tried to dry it with the hand dryer in the men's room, but it just set the stain. It was this awful yellow tie with little blue anchors on it. I don't know why he likes anchors so much, he doesn't even own a boat. 

My uncle had a boat, though. It was a pontoon boat that he kept up at the lake. We used to go up there every summer when I was a kid. We’d go tubing behind it, and my cousins would always try to flip my tube over on purpose. One time, my cousin Ricky flipped me so hard I lost my favorite pair of sunglasses in the lake. They were those cheap neon pink ones that were popular in the eighties, but I loved them. Ricky felt so bad he bought me a snow cone from the marina to make up for it. The marina had the best snow cones, they used real cane sugar syrup instead of that artificial stuff. They had this flavor called "Tiger's Blood" that was coconut and strawberry, and it turned your tongue bright red for hours. 

Wait... what was I saying? Oh, right, trouble keeping up with things. Yeah, I'm just really struggling to handle work and the house stuff right now. It's just all too much.

I used to bag groceries when I was in high school, back at this little independent market that isn't even there anymore—they tore it down to build that new apartment complex, the one with the weird modern balconies? They look like they're going to fall off, honestly. I don't know who approved that design. \n\nBut anyway, I just... I snapped at her. I told her she was going to ruin my groceries and I grabbed the bags and practically ran out of the store. I was so embarrassed. But I just couldn't stop myself. My husband even said something about it yesterday when I got mad about the dishwasher not being loaded right. He always puts the bowls on the bottom rack, and they never get clean that way. I've told him a million times. It's just basic physics, the water needs to reach the inside... but yeah, I just exploded at him. It's bad. It's really bad.\n\nDoctor: When you feel yourself getting angry like that, do you notice any other physical symptoms — like a racing heart, sweating, or feeling really restless?\n\nPatient: Physical symptoms? They keep telling me they put in a work order with this local HVAC company, but I actually know the owner of that company—his daughter used to take dance classes with my niece—and he said they never even got a call from our corporate office. It's just ridiculous. We have these tiny little plastic desk fans at the registers, which do absolutely nothing when it's ninety degrees out. \n\nAnd as for feeling restless... I don't know, I drink a lot of coffee, so I guess I'm always a little wired anyway. There’s this new drive-thru coffee place that just opened up right next to our plaza. I tried their iced caramel thing once and it was way too sweet. Like drinking straight syrup. I don't know how people drink that every morning.\n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. It's this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. \n\nAnd the neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's sweet but she always leaves her recycling bins out for like three or four days after pickup, and the wind blows the plastic bottles all over the street. The HOA sent out a letter about it last month, but they print everything on this really cheap, flimsy paper that just gets stuck in the mailbox hinges. \n\nBut anyway, I wasn't mad about any of that yesterday. I was just enjoying the morning and watering the plants. So, no, I don't feel irritable constantly... it just sort of bubbles up and explodes out of nowhere when those specific moments happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens in the time between these outbursts? Do you ever feel guilty, ashamed, or regretful after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, God, yes. It's awful. Like I said, I'm really here because I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's so out of character for me. Like last week, I yelled at one of my new employees for putting the backstock on the wrong shelf in the stockroom. I mean, I really let her have it. And afterward, I went into the breakroom and I was literally shaking, just trembling all over, because that is not how I usually treat people. I've always prided myself on being a good manager. \n\nWhen I first started in retail, my manager was this woman named Brenda, and she was a nightmare. She used to make us clock out and then stay to sweep the floors, which I'm pretty sure is highly illegal. I tried to tell my district manager about it, but he was always at these corporate retreats in Florida playing golf. They always go to Florida, even in the middle of summer when it's just a swamp. My sister went to Disney World in July once, and she said her kids were just melting on the pavement. They spent half their vacation money on those little battery-operated fan misters that break after two days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I feel terrible after I snap. Just so ashamed. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. They have the right lane completely blocked off with those orange barrels, and they've been sitting there for three weeks and I haven't seen a single worker actually doing anything. They just leave the heavy machinery parked on the grass median. \n\nMy husband says it's because the city ran out of funding for the project halfway through, which wouldn't surprise me at all. Our property taxes went up again this year, and for what? Potholes? We have this one pothole at the entrance of our neighborhood that is so deep, I swear it's going to rip the axle right off my Honda. I drive a CR-V, which I usually love, but the newer models have this weird touchscreen thing for the radio that is so distracting when you're trying to drive. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road. Now you have to tap this little screen three times just to turn down the air conditioning. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no, my emotions are fine otherwise. It's just... it's just those sudden moments where I just lose it. The rest of the time I feel pretty normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have any of these outbursts led to serious consequences, like problems at work, relationship issues, or legal trouble?\n\nPatient: Serious consequences? Um, yeah. Yeah, it's definitely causing problems. I mean, I've completely missed a vendor meeting because I was just too overwhelmed, and the other day I literally just walked out before my shift was even over. I just couldn't do it. And on top of that, I can't even bring myself to go back to the house to deal with the damage, so my work stuff and my home responsibilities are just piling up way faster than I can handle. \n\nWhich is stressing my husband out, too. He’s been trying to deal with the insurance adjuster, but those guys are impossible to get on the phone. They always put you on hold with that awful elevator music. I don't understand why companies can't just play normal music, or at least a podcast or something while you wait. My nephew started a podcast last year—something about retro video games. He bought all this expensive microphone equipment and set up a whole studio in his parents' basement. My sister was furious because he took down all her old sewing tables to make room for it. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. She made one for my cousin's baby shower, but my cousin ended up moving to Arizona, and who needs a heavy quilt in Arizona? It’s like 110 degrees there in the shade. \n\nBut, um, yeah... my responsibilities are just... they're completely piling up. Work and home. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: During these outbursts, do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions or have trouble remembering what happened while you were angry?\n\nPatient: Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word. \n\nSpeaking of remembering things, my memory has been terrible lately with groceries. I went to the store yesterday specifically for coffee filters and milk, and I walked out with two bags of potting soil and a new garden hose. I don't even have a garden anymore. We used to have this beautiful little raised bed in the backyard where I grew cherry tomatoes, but the squirrels kept getting to them before they were ripe. Squirrels are so smart, you know? My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" bird feeders—the ones that spin around when something heavy lands on them. He paid like eighty dollars for it at that fancy wild bird store downtown. The store is right next to this little Italian bakery that makes the most incredible cannolis. They fill them right when you order so the shell doesn't get soggy. I brought a box to work once for a birthday, and they were gone in five minutes. \n\nBut anyway, no, I... I remember the outbursts perfectly. I'm definitely in control of my actions, I'm just... I'm just really angry in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever find yourself feeling persistently angry or irritable, even if you’re not lashing out?\n\nPatient: Persistently angry? No... no, I wouldn't say that. Once I calm down from one of those moments, I'm just kind of back to normal. I'm not walking around irritable all day. I actually try to keep things pretty peaceful. \n\nI even started drinking chamomile tea in the evenings to just, you know, relax. Have you ever tried the loose-leaf kind? My coworker Brenda swears by it. She buys it from this little holistic shop a few towns over. Honestly, it smells a bit like dried grass to me, but Brenda is super into all that natural wellness stuff lately. She even started making her own soap out of goat's milk. She bought two actual pygmy goats last spring to keep in her backyard! They are adorable, but they literally eat everything in sight. They chewed right through her husband's favorite pair of gardening gloves that he left on the patio. He was furious because they were those expensive, thick leather ones you have to special order online. He uses them for his rose bushes. He grows those really big heirloom roses, the ones that smell amazing but have thorns like daggers. I tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles absolutely decimated them in like three days. They just turn the leaves into lace, it's awful. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, being irritable all the time. No, I don't just sit around feeling angry. It's really just those sudden blow-ups, and then I'm fine. Well, not fine, but not angry.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever feel unusually sad, anxious, or emotionally sensitive — like small things affect your mood more than they used to?\n\nPatient: Unusually sad or sensitive? Um, no, not really. I don't think little things are getting to me more than usual. I'm not, like, crying at commercials or anything. Though, honestly, I haven't even watched much TV lately because the remote at the temporary place we're staying is completely broken. The buttons just stick. It's one of those cheap universal remotes, which I always find so confusing anyway. \n\nMy dad used to have this massive universal remote in the nineties, it was practically the size of a textbook. He was so proud of it because it could control the VCR and the stereo at the exact same time. He used to listen to all these old jazz cassettes on that stereo. Mostly Miles Davis and John Coltrane. He actually tried to teach me how to play the saxophone when I was in middle school, but I was terrible at it. You have to blow into it so hard, it made my cheeks ache. Plus, the carrying case weighed a ton, and I had to lug it all the way to school on the bus. The bus driver, Mr. Abernathy, was this really grumpy guy who would yell if you even whispered. He actually kicked my friend Sarah off the bus once just for blowing a bubble with her bubblegum. Can you believe that? She had to walk two miles home in the rain. Her mom was so mad she called the school board and threatened to sue the district. \n\nWait, I'm sorry, I lost track again. What did you just ask me? Oh, right, feeling sad or anxious. Yeah, no, I don't really feel like that. I'm not walking around weeping or feeling super sensitive or anything. Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but that's mostly just from work. Standing behind a register for eight hours really does a number on your arches, you know? I actually had to buy these special orthopedic inserts last week. They cost like forty dollars at the pharmacy down the street. \n\nThe pharmacist there, Dave, he’s been working there since I was a teenager. I guess his daughter-in-law knits them. I tried knitting once a couple of years ago, when everyone was stuck at home making sourdough bread and doing crafts. I bought all this expensive merino wool yarn online, but it got completely tangled and my cat ended up just dragging it under the sofa. She’s a rescue, part Siamese, so she’s incredibly vocal. She just yowls at the bedroom door at three in the morning if her food bowl is even slightly empty. It’s not even that she’s actually hungry, she just hates seeing the bottom of the bowl. It was this awful yellow tie with little blue anchors on it. I don't know why he likes anchors so much, he doesn't even own a boat. \n\nMy uncle had a boat, though. Ricky felt so bad he bought me a snow cone from the marina to make up for it. The marina had the best snow cones, they used real cane sugar syrup instead of that artificial stuff. Feeling down or hopeless? Um, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. I just... I haven't really been doing much of anything I usually enjoy, honestly. I mean, I've completely missed a vendor meeting because I was just too overwhelmed, and the other day I literally just walked out before my shift was even over. I just couldn't do it. And on top of that, I can't even bring myself to go back to the house to deal with the damage, so my work stuff and my home responsibilities are just piling up way faster than I can handle. \n\nWhich is stressing my husband out, too. He’s been trying to deal with the insurance adjuster, but those guys are impossible to get on the phone. They always put you on hold with that awful elevator music. I don't understand why companies can't just play normal music, or at least a podcast or something while you wait. My nephew started a podcast last year—something about retro video games. He bought all this expensive microphone equipment and set up a whole studio in his parents' basement. My sister was furious because he took down all her old sewing tables to make room for it. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. She made one for my cousin's baby shower, but my cousin ended up moving to Arizona, and who needs a heavy quilt in Arizona? It’s like 110 degrees there in the shade. \n\nBut, um, yeah... my responsibilities are just... they're completely piling up. Work and home. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: During these outbursts, do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions or have trouble remembering what happened while you were angry?\n\nPatient: Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word. \n\nSpeaking of remembering things, my memory has been terrible lately with groceries. I went to the store yesterday specifically for coffee filters and milk, and I walked out with two bags of potting soil and a new garden hose. I don't even have a garden anymore. We used to have this beautiful little raised bed in the backyard where I grew cherry tomatoes, but the squirrels kept getting to them before they were ripe. Squirrels are so smart, you know? My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" bird feeders—the ones that spin around when something heavy lands on them. He paid like eighty dollars for it at that fancy wild bird store downtown. The store is right next to this little Italian bakery that makes the most incredible cannolis. They fill them right when you order so the shell doesn't get soggy. I brought a box to work once for a birthday, and they were gone in five minutes. \n\nBut anyway, no, I... I remember the outbursts perfectly. I'm definitely in control of my actions, I'm just... I'm just really angry in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever find yourself feeling persistently angry or irritable, even if you’re not lashing out?\n\nPatient: Persistently angry? No... no, I wouldn't say that. Once I calm down from one of those moments, I'm just kind of back to normal. I'm not walking around irritable all day. I actually try to keep things pretty peaceful. \n\nI even started drinking chamomile tea in the evenings to just, you know, relax. Have you ever tried the loose-leaf kind? My coworker Brenda swears by it. She buys it from this little holistic shop a few towns over. Honestly, it smells a bit like dried grass to me, but Brenda is super into all that natural wellness stuff lately. She even started making her own soap out of goat's milk. She bought two actual pygmy goats last spring to keep in her backyard! They are adorable, but they literally eat everything in sight. They chewed right through her husband's favorite pair of gardening gloves that he left on the patio. He was furious because they were those expensive, thick leather ones you have to special order online. He uses them for his rose bushes. He grows those really big heirloom roses, the ones that smell amazing but have thorns like daggers. I tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles absolutely decimated them in like three days. They just turn the leaves into lace, it's awful. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, being irritable all the time. No, I don't just sit around feeling angry. Um, no, not really. I don't think little things are getting to me more than usual. I'm not, like, crying at commercials or anything. Though, honestly, I haven't even watched much TV lately because the remote at the temporary place we're staying is completely broken. The buttons just stick. It's one of those cheap universal remotes, which I always find so confusing anyway. \n\nMy dad used to have this massive universal remote in the nineties, it was practically the size of a textbook. He was so proud of it because it could control the VCR and the stereo at the exact same time. He used to listen to all these old jazz cassettes on that stereo. Mostly Miles Davis and John Coltrane. He actually tried to teach me how to play the saxophone when I was in middle school, but I was terrible at it. You have to blow into it so hard, it made my cheeks ache. Plus, the carrying case weighed a ton, and I had to lug it all the way to school on the bus. The bus driver, Mr. Abernathy, was this really grumpy guy who would yell if you even whispered. He actually kicked my friend Sarah off the bus once just for blowing a bubble with her bubblegum. Can you believe that? She had to walk two miles home in the rain. Her mom was so mad she called the school board and threatened to sue the district. \n\nWait, I'm sorry, I lost track again. What did you just ask me? Oh, right, feeling sad or anxious. Yeah, no, I don't really feel like that. I'm not walking around weeping or feeling super sensitive or anything. I bought all this expensive merino wool yarn online, but it got completely tangled and my cat ended up just dragging it under the sofa. She’s a rescue, part Siamese, so she’s incredibly vocal. She just yowls at the bedroom door at three in the morning if her food bowl is even slightly empty. It’s not even that she’s actually hungry, she just hates seeing the bottom of the bowl. It was this awful yellow tie with little blue anchors on it. I don't know why he likes anchors so much, he doesn't even own a boat. \n\nMy uncle had a boat, though. It was a pontoon boat that he kept up at the lake. We used to go up there every summer when I was a kid. We’d go tubing behind it, and my cousins would always try to flip my tube over on purpose. One time, my cousin Ricky flipped me so hard I lost my favorite pair of sunglasses in the lake. They were those cheap neon pink ones that were popular in the eighties, but I loved them. Ricky felt so bad he bought me a snow cone from the marina to make up for it. The marina had the best snow cones, they used real cane sugar syrup instead of that artificial stuff. Now you have to tap this little screen three times just to turn down the air conditioning. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no, my emotions are fine otherwise. It's just... it's just those sudden moments where I just lose it. The rest of the time I feel pretty normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have any of these outbursts led to serious consequences, like problems at work, relationship issues, or legal trouble?\n\nPatient: Serious consequences? Um, yeah. Yeah, it's definitely causing problems. I mean, I've completely missed a vendor meeting because I was just too overwhelmed, and the other day I literally just walked out before my shift was even over. I just couldn't do it. And on top of that, I can't even bring myself to go back to the house to deal with the damage, so my work stuff and my home responsibilities are just piling up way faster than I can handle. \n\nWhich is stressing my husband out, too. He’s been trying to deal with the insurance adjuster, but those guys are impossible to get on the phone. They always put you on hold with that awful elevator music. I don't understand why companies can't just play normal music, or at least a podcast or something while you wait. My nephew started a podcast last year—something about retro video games. He bought all this expensive microphone equipment and set up a whole studio in his parents' basement. My sister was furious because he took down all her old sewing tables to make room for it. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. She made one for my cousin's baby shower, but my cousin ended up moving to Arizona, and who needs a heavy quilt in Arizona? It’s like 110 degrees there in the shade. \n\nBut, um, yeah... my responsibilities are just... they're completely piling up. Work and home. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: During these outbursts, do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions or have trouble remembering what happened while you were angry?\n\nPatient: Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word. \n\nSpeaking of remembering things, my memory has been terrible lately with groceries. I went to the store yesterday specifically for coffee filters and milk, and I walked out with two bags of potting soil and a new garden hose. I don't even have a garden anymore. We used to have this beautiful little raised bed in the backyard where I grew cherry tomatoes, but the squirrels kept getting to them before they were ripe. Squirrels are so smart, you know? My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" bird feeders—the ones that spin around when something heavy lands on them. He paid like eighty dollars for it at that fancy wild bird store downtown. The store is right next to this little Italian bakery that makes the most incredible cannolis. They fill them right when you order so the shell doesn't get soggy. I brought a box to work once for a birthday, and they were gone in five minutes. \n\nBut anyway, no, I... I remember the outbursts perfectly. I'm definitely in control of my actions, I'm just... I'm just really angry in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever find yourself feeling persistently angry or irritable, even if you’re not lashing out?\n\nPatient: Persistently angry? I actually try to keep things pretty peaceful. \n\nI even started drinking chamomile tea in the evenings to just, you know, relax. Have you ever tried the loose-leaf kind? Honestly, it smells a bit like dried grass to me, but Brenda is super into all that natural wellness stuff lately. She even started making her own soap out of goat's milk. She bought two actual pygmy goats last spring to keep in her backyard! They are adorable, but they literally eat everything in sight. They chewed right through her husband's favorite pair of gardening gloves that he left on the patio. He grows those really big heirloom roses, the ones that smell amazing but have thorns like daggers. I tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles absolutely decimated them in like three days. They just turn the leaves into lace, it's awful. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, being irritable all the time. No, I don't just sit around feeling angry. It's really just those sudden blow-ups, and then I'm fine. Um, no, not really. I don't think little things are getting to me more than usual. I'm not, like, crying at commercials or anything. Though, honestly, I haven't even watched much TV lately because the remote at the temporary place we're staying is completely broken. The buttons just stick. It's one of those cheap universal remotes, which I always find so confusing anyway. \n\nMy dad used to have this massive universal remote in the nineties, it was practically the size of a textbook. He was so proud of it because it could control the VCR and the stereo at the exact same time. He used to listen to all these old jazz cassettes on that stereo. Mostly Miles Davis and John Coltrane. I bought all this expensive merino wool yarn online, but it got completely tangled and my cat ended up just dragging it under the sofa. She’s a rescue, part Siamese, so she’s incredibly vocal. She just yowls at the bedroom door at three in the morning if her food bowl is even slightly empty. It’s not even that she’s actually hungry, she just hates seeing the bottom of the bowl. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Relief? No, definitely not. It's actually the opposite. That's why I'm here, honestly. I keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. Like, I yelled at one of my employees for putting stock on the wrong shelf, and afterward, I was literally shaking because that is just not how I usually treat people. It felt awful. 

Usually, I'm the one who's super patient with the new hires. We had this one kid, Tommy, who started last summer. He was so nervous on his first day that he dropped a whole pallet of paint cans in the receiving area. It looked like a modern art masterpiece exploded back there. Mostly "Navajo White" and "Seafoam Green." Seafoam is such a weird color name, isn't it? It doesn't really look like foam from the sea. Sea foam is usually just dirty white bubbles with seaweed mixed in. I went to the beach in Florida once, Clearwater, and the water was gorgeous, but the foam was kind of gross. There were these little sand crabs everywhere, too. My sister stepped on one and screamed so loud the lifeguard blew his whistle at her. She was always so dramatic. She once cried for three days because her favorite boy band broke up. I think it was NSYNC? Or maybe the Backstreet Boys. She had posters covering every inch of her bedroom walls, you couldn't even see the wallpaper. It was this hideous floral wallpaper my mom picked out in the nineties, so the posters were probably an improvement. 

Wait, what did you ask me? Relief? Right, no. I don't feel relieved at all. I just feel shaky and terrible after I lose my temper like that.

Corporate sent down these new metal racks last month and they don't even fit the endcaps properly, so the boxes are always sliding around. I told my district manager about it, but he never listens, he just sends more emails about our numbers. \n\nBut anyway, I just screamed at this poor kid over the stock, and afterward I had to go sit in the back room because I was just shaking. Literally shaking. Because that is not how I usually treat people. I'm usually very patient with my team. \n\nThe traffic getting to your office was awful, by the way. They have Main Street completely torn up for that water main thing, and the detour signs make no sense. I almost missed the turn into the parking lot. But yeah, the yelling... I just can't keep snapping at people like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you've had other episodes like this, where your emotions felt completely out of control, and how often they tend to happen?\n\nPatient: Um, other episodes... well, yeah, I guess. It's been happening a lot lately. Just, like, zero to sixty, you know? Like last week, I was at the grocery store, just trying to get some dinner after a double shift. And the cashier was moving so slow. I mean, painfully slow. And they were bagging my bread with the canned goods! Who does that? I used to bag groceries when I was in high school, back at this little independent market that isn't even there anymore—they tore it down to build that new apartment complex, the one with the weird modern balconies? They look like they're going to fall off, honestly. I don't know who approved that design. \n\nBut anyway, I just... I snapped at her. I told her she was going to ruin my groceries and I grabbed the bags and practically ran out of the store. I was so embarrassed. But I just couldn't stop myself. It's like I'm just so on edge all the time now. I don't know exactly how often it's happening, but it feels like it's all the time. My husband even said something about it yesterday when I got mad about the dishwasher not being loaded right. He always puts the bowls on the bottom rack, and they never get clean that way. I've told him a million times. It's just basic physics, the water needs to reach the inside... but yeah, I just exploded at him. It's bad. They keep telling me they put in a work order with this local HVAC company, but I actually know the owner of that company—his daughter used to take dance classes with my niece—and he said they never even got a call from our corporate office. It's just ridiculous. We have these tiny little plastic desk fans at the registers, which do absolutely nothing when it's ninety degrees out. \n\nAnd as for feeling restless... I don't know, I drink a lot of coffee, so I guess I'm always a little wired anyway. There’s this new drive-thru coffee place that just opened up right next to our plaza. The line is always spilling out onto the main road, which makes getting into our parking lot a total nightmare. People will literally just sit there blocking traffic for a seven-dollar latte. I tried their iced caramel thing once and it was way too sweet. Like drinking straight syrup. I don't know how people drink that every morning.\n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, when I snap at people. No, I don't really notice my heart racing or sweating right then. Just that shaking thing I told you about afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find yourself feeling consistently irritable or angry most of the time?\n\nPatient: Am I angry all the time? Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. So, no, I don't feel irritable constantly... it just sort of bubbles up and explodes out of nowhere when those specific moments happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens in the time between these outbursts? Do you ever feel guilty, ashamed, or regretful after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, God, yes. It's awful. Like I said, I'm really here because I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's so out of character for me. Like last week, I yelled at one of my new employees for putting the backstock on the wrong shelf in the stockroom. I mean, I really let her have it. And afterward, I went into the breakroom and I was literally shaking, just trembling all over, because that is not how I usually treat people. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road.
