No... no, I wouldn't say that. Once I calm down from one of those moments, I'm just kind of back to normal. I'm not walking around irritable all day. I actually try to keep things pretty peaceful. \n\nI even started drinking chamomile tea in the evenings to just, you know, relax. Have you ever tried the loose-leaf kind? My coworker Brenda swears by it. She buys it from this little holistic shop a few towns over. Honestly, it smells a bit like dried grass to me, but Brenda is super into all that natural wellness stuff lately. She even started making her own soap out of goat's milk. She bought two actual pygmy goats last spring to keep in her backyard! They are adorable, but they literally eat everything in sight. They chewed right through her husband's favorite pair of gardening gloves that he left on the patio. He was furious because they were those expensive, thick leather ones you have to special order online. He uses them for his rose bushes. He grows those really big heirloom roses, the ones that smell amazing but have thorns like daggers. I tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles absolutely decimated them in like three days. They just turn the leaves into lace, it's awful. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, being irritable all the time. No, I don't just sit around feeling angry. It's really just those sudden blow-ups, and then I'm fine. Well, not fine, but not angry.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you ever feel unusually sad, anxious, or emotionally sensitive — like small things affect your mood more than they used to?\n\nPatient: Unusually sad or sensitive? Um, no, not really. I don't think little things are getting to me more than usual. I'm not, like, crying at commercials or anything. Though, honestly, I haven't even watched much TV lately because the remote at the temporary place we're staying is completely broken. The buttons just stick. It's one of those cheap universal remotes, which I always find so confusing anyway. \n\nMy dad used to have this massive universal remote in the nineties, it was practically the size of a textbook. He was so proud of it because it could control the VCR and the stereo at the exact same time. He used to listen to all these old jazz cassettes on that stereo. Mostly Miles Davis and John Coltrane. He actually tried to teach me how to play the saxophone when I was in middle school, but I was terrible at it. You have to blow into it so hard, it made my cheeks ache. Plus, the carrying case weighed a ton, and I had to lug it all the way to school on the bus. The bus driver, Mr. Abernathy, was this really grumpy guy who would yell if you even whispered. He actually kicked my friend Sarah off the bus once just for blowing a bubble with her bubblegum. Can you believe that? She had to walk two miles home in the rain. Her mom was so mad she called the school board and threatened to sue the district. \n\nWait, I'm sorry, I lost track again. What did you just ask me? Oh, right, feeling sad or anxious. Yeah, no, I don't really feel like that. I'm not walking around weeping or feeling super sensitive or anything. Between those angry moments, I'm pretty much just my normal self.\n\nDoctor: After one of these angry outbursts, do you ever feel unusually down, sensitive, or emotionally drained?\n\nPatient: Drained? Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but that's mostly just from work. Standing behind a register for eight hours really does a number on your arches, you know? I actually had to buy these special orthopedic inserts last week. They cost like forty dollars at the pharmacy down the street. \n\nThe pharmacist there, Dave, he’s been working there since I was a teenager. He actually just became a grandfather for the third time and he was showing me pictures of the baby on his phone. The baby was wearing this tiny little hand-knit sweater with ducks on it. I guess his daughter-in-law knits them. I tried knitting once a couple of years ago, when everyone was stuck at home making sourdough bread and doing crafts. I bought all this expensive merino wool yarn online, but it got completely tangled and my cat ended up just dragging it under the sofa. She’s a rescue, part Siamese, so she’s incredibly vocal. She just yowls at the bedroom door at three in the morning if her food bowl is even slightly empty. It’s not even that she’s actually hungry, she just hates seeing the bottom of the bowl. Cats are so weird about that, aren't they? My friend's cat actually refuses to drink out of a bowl at all and will only drink from a running faucet. \n\nAnyway... wait, I'm doing it again. What did you ask me? Oh, right, feeling down or drained after I get mad. No, I don't really get depressed or overly sensitive afterward. I just kind of want to move past it and get back to whatever I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Outside of the moments when you're having these angry outbursts, have you noticed any difficulty functioning in your daily life — like trouble concentrating, making decisions, or taking care of responsibilities at home or work?\n\nPatient: Trouble functioning? Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. I actually missed a vendor meeting last week, which is really unlike me. And a couple of days ago, I just walked out before my shift was even over. I couldn't take being in the store anymore. Plus, I just... I can't face going back to the damaged house right now to deal with any of it, so all my home stuff and my work responsibilities are just piling up faster than I can handle them. \n\nMy manager at work, Gary, he was pretty upset when I walked out. He’s one of those guys who takes retail very, very seriously, you know? Like, he wears a tie every single day, even though it’s just a hardware store and half the time we’re covered in dust from the lumber aisle. He actually spilled coffee down his tie the other morning and tried to dry it with the hand dryer in the men's room, but it just set the stain. It was this awful yellow tie with little blue anchors on it. I don't know why he likes anchors so much, he doesn't even own a boat. \n\nMy uncle had a boat, though. It was a pontoon boat that he kept up at the lake. We used to go up there every summer when I was a kid. We’d go tubing behind it, and my cousins would always try to flip my tube over on purpose. One time, my cousin Ricky flipped me so hard I lost my favorite pair of sunglasses in the lake. They were those cheap neon pink ones that were popular in the eighties, but I loved them. Ricky felt so bad he bought me a snow cone from the marina to make up for it. The marina had the best snow cones, they used real cane sugar syrup instead of that artificial stuff. They had this flavor called \"Tiger's Blood\" that was coconut and strawberry, and it turned your tongue bright red for hours. \n\nWait... what was I saying? Oh, right, trouble keeping up with things. Yeah, I'm just really struggling to handle work and the house stuff right now. It's just all too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down, losing interest in things you usually enjoy, or feeling hopeless about your situation lately?\n\nPatient: Feeling down or hopeless? Um, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. I just... I haven't really been doing much of anything I usually enjoy, honestly. But that's mostly because everything is just so chaotic right now. I usually love baking on my days off, but all my good baking pans are still at the house, probably ruined. \n\nI had this really nice heavy-duty stand mixer, the kind they use on those baking shows. I saved up for like six months to buy it. It's this beautiful retro mint green color. I actually picked that color because it matched the vintage Pyrex bowls my grandmother left me. She had the whole primary colors set, the nesting ones? Those things are virtually indestructible. She used to make the most incredible potato salad in the big yellow one every Fourth of July. She always put chopped up pickles in it, but not the sweet kind, only dill. My grandfather absolutely hated sweet pickles. He said they were an abomination. He was a very stubborn man. He actually refused to buy a microwave until the late nineties because he was convinced they leaked radiation and would mutate the dog. They had this little terrier mix named Buster who would just sit by the oven waiting for food to drop. Buster was so fat he looked like a furry footstool. \n\nWait, sorry. I'm rambling again, aren't I? What did you ask me? Hopeless? Right, no. I don't feel hopeless or depressed. I'm just... existing right now, trying to get through the days. But I'm not sitting around feeling sorry for myself or anything.\n\nDoctor: Between these angry episodes, have you found yourself not caring about things you normally care about, or not enjoying activities you used to like?\n\nPatient: Not caring about things? I mean, I guess I just answered that, didn't I? I haven't really been doing much of anything I usually like. Like I said, the baking stuff is all at the house. And I just... I don't really have the energy to care about much else right now. \n\nIt's kind of like how I used to feel about my garden. I had this amazing vegetable garden in my backyard a few years ago. I grew these massive heirloom tomatoes, the purple Cherokee kind? They are so ugly, all lumpy and weird-looking, but they taste incredible. I used to make fresh salsa every weekend. I even tried growing my own jalapeños, but I planted them too close to the bell peppers and they cross-pollinated or something, so my bell peppers ended up being spicy. I didn't know that could happen until I bit into one like an apple and my eyes started watering. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she saw me coughing in the yard and thought I was choking. She came running over with her garden hose, ready to spray me in the face. She's a sweet lady, but a little intense. She used to be a gym teacher in the seventies, and she still wears those tracksuits, you know the ones that make that swishing sound when you walk? She has them in every color of the rainbow. \n\nWait, what was the question? Not caring about things? Yeah, I don't know, I just... I haven't really been doing my normal stuff. But I wouldn't say I don't care at all, it's just hard to focus on hobbies right now with everything else going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a sense of relief or release during or immediately after one of these angry outbursts?\n\nPatient: Relief? It felt awful. \n\nUsually, I'm the one who's super patient with the new hires. We had this one kid, Tommy, who started last summer. She had posters covering every inch of her bedroom walls, you couldn't even see the wallpaper. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not enjoying things? I mean, I guess it's like I said before. I just don't have the energy for it right now. Even when I'm not feeling actively stressed or yelling at someone, I'm just kind of... flat. 

I used to love going to the farmers market on Saturday mornings, even if I didn't need anything. Just walking around, looking at the stalls. There was this one guy who sold the most amazing local honey. He had all these different varieties depending on what the bees were pollinating. The orange blossom one was incredible. He had this whole setup with a glass hive where you could actually see the bees working. It's fascinating how they all have specific jobs, you know? Like the worker bees and the drones. I watched this documentary once about how they communicate through dancing. They do this little figure-eight waggle dance to tell the other bees where the good flowers are. It's wild that insects can be that smart. My cousin had an ant farm when we were kids, one of those plastic ones with the green gel inside instead of dirt? He got it for his birthday, but he left it sitting in the sun on his windowsill and they all kind of... baked. It was really sad, actually. Um, no, I don't really think so. Like, I'm not just walking around fuming all day. Yesterday morning, for example, I was perfectly fine. I was actually out in the front yard watering my hydrangeas. They are doing so well this year, which is honestly a miracle because the soil in our subdivision is basically just solid clay. When we first moved in, my husband tried to plant these expensive heirloom tomatoes he ordered online, and they completely died within a week. He tried to blame the neighbor's cat, which is just ridiculous because that cat is like fifteen years old and barely even leaves its porch. It's this huge orange tabby named Marmalade. \n\nAnd the neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she's sweet but she always leaves her recycling bins out for like three or four days after pickup, and the wind blows the plastic bottles all over the street. The HOA sent out a letter about it last month, but they print everything on this really cheap, flimsy paper that just gets stuck in the mailbox hinges. \n\nBut anyway, I wasn't mad about any of that yesterday. I was just enjoying the morning and watering the plants. So, no, I don't feel irritable constantly... it just sort of bubbles up and explodes out of nowhere when those specific moments happen.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens in the time between these outbursts? Do you ever feel guilty, ashamed, or regretful after these episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, God, yes. It's awful. Like I said, I'm really here because I just keep blowing up at people over absolutely nothing. It's so out of character for me. Like last week, I yelled at one of my new employees for putting the backstock on the wrong shelf in the stockroom. I mean, I really let her have it. And afterward, I went into the breakroom and I was literally shaking, just trembling all over, because that is not how I usually treat people. I've always prided myself on being a good manager. \n\nWhen I first started in retail, my manager was this woman named Brenda, and she was a nightmare. She used to make us clock out and then stay to sweep the floors, which I'm pretty sure is highly illegal. I tried to tell my district manager about it, but he was always at these corporate retreats in Florida playing golf. They always go to Florida, even in the middle of summer when it's just a swamp. My sister went to Disney World in July once, and she said her kids were just melting on the pavement. They spent half their vacation money on those little battery-operated fan misters that break after two days. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I feel terrible after I snap. Just so ashamed. I don't want to be that kind of person, the kind of boss who makes people cry over a misplaced box of merchandise. It just... it happens, and then I feel horrible about it.\n\nDoctor: Between these outbursts, do you find it hard to control or manage your emotions in other situations, or do things usually feel stable emotionally until one of these episodes happens?\n\nPatient: Stable? Yeah, I'd say I'm pretty stable between the blow-ups. Like, I don't really have trouble managing my emotions most of the time. I'm usually the one keeping everyone else calm, especially at work. You know, retail is just a circus anyway. Especially right now because they're doing all this road construction right in front of our strip mall. They have the right lane completely blocked off with those orange barrels, and they've been sitting there for three weeks and I haven't seen a single worker actually doing anything. They just leave the heavy machinery parked on the grass median. \n\nMy husband says it's because the city ran out of funding for the project halfway through, which wouldn't surprise me at all. Our property taxes went up again this year, and for what? Potholes? We have this one pothole at the entrance of our neighborhood that is so deep, I swear it's going to rip the axle right off my Honda. I drive a CR-V, which I usually love, but the newer models have this weird touchscreen thing for the radio that is so distracting when you're trying to drive. I miss the old physical buttons, you know? You could just feel for the volume knob without taking your eyes off the road. Now you have to tap this little screen three times just to turn down the air conditioning. \n\nBut, um... yeah, no, my emotions are fine otherwise. It's just... it's just those sudden moments where I just lose it. Um, yeah. Yeah, it's definitely causing problems. I mean, I've completely missed a vendor meeting because I was just too overwhelmed, and the other day I literally just walked out before my shift was even over. I just couldn't do it. And on top of that, I can't even bring myself to go back to the house to deal with the damage, so my work stuff and my home responsibilities are just piling up way faster than I can handle. \n\nWhich is stressing my husband out, too. She used to make these beautiful quilts, like, really intricate ones with little embroidered birds on them. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: During these outbursts, do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions or have trouble remembering what happened while you were angry?\n\nPatient: Control? No, I don't... I don't think I lose control like that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing and saying when I'm yelling, and I remember all of it afterward. That's why I feel so awful. It's not like I black out or anything. I remember every stupid word. \n\nSpeaking of remembering things, my memory has been terrible lately with groceries. I went to the store yesterday specifically for coffee filters and milk, and I walked out with two bags of potting soil and a new garden hose. I don't even have a garden anymore. We used to have this beautiful little raised bed in the backyard where I grew cherry tomatoes, but the squirrels kept getting to them before they were ripe. Squirrels are so smart, you know? My neighbor, Mr. Henderson, bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" bird feeders—the ones that spin around when something heavy lands on them. He paid like eighty dollars for it at that fancy wild bird store downtown. The store is right next to this little Italian bakery that makes the most incredible cannolis. They fill them right when you order so the shell doesn't get soggy. I brought a box to work once for a birthday, and they were gone in five minutes. \n\nBut anyway, no, I... I remember the outbursts perfectly. I'm definitely in control of my actions, I'm just... I actually try to keep things pretty peaceful. \n\nI even started drinking chamomile tea in the evenings to just, you know, relax. Have you ever tried the loose-leaf kind? My coworker Brenda swears by it. She buys it from this little holistic shop a few towns over. Honestly, it smells a bit like dried grass to me, but Brenda is super into all that natural wellness stuff lately. She even started making her own soap out of goat's milk. She bought two actual pygmy goats last spring to keep in her backyard! They are adorable, but they literally eat everything in sight. They chewed right through her husband's favorite pair of gardening gloves that he left on the patio. He was furious because they were those expensive, thick leather ones you have to special order online. He uses them for his rose bushes. They just turn the leaves into lace, it's awful. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, being irritable all the time. No, I don't just sit around feeling angry. He was so proud of it because it could control the VCR and the stereo at the exact same time. He used to listen to all these old jazz cassettes on that stereo. Mostly Miles Davis and John Coltrane. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Didn't you just ask me that? Or something really similar? I feel like I just answered this. Like I said, I'm just flat. I don't really take pleasure in much right now, even if I'm not actively mad about something. 

It's just hard to care. Kind of like when you have a cold and you can't taste your food, so eating just feels like a chore. I had COVID back in 2021 and I lost my sense of smell and taste for almost a month. It was the weirdest thing. I tried eating a raw onion just to see if I could taste it, and it was just crunchy water. My husband thought I was crazy. He filmed me doing it and put it on Facebook, which I was pretty annoyed about because I was wearing my ratty old bathrobe. It's this ugly pink fleece thing, but it's so warm. I bought it at a thrift store in college for like three dollars. Thrift stores used to be so cheap, but now everyone goes there and they mark everything up. I saw a used candle there once. Half burned! Who buys a half-burned candle? 

Anyway, yeah, I guess I just don't really take pleasure in my normal stuff right now. It all just feels a bit pointless.

He got it for his birthday, but he left it sitting in the sun on his windowsill and they all kind of... baked. It was really sad, actually. He cried for like an hour, and then my aunt bought him a goldfish to make up for it. He named the goldfish \"Ant.\" \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? The farmers market. Yeah, I just... I haven't been going. Even on days when I feel okay, I just don't really want to do the things I used to like.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, even on days when you’re not feeling stressed or angry?\n\nPatient: Didn't you just ask me that? Or something really similar? I feel like I just answered this. Like I said, I'm just flat. I don't really take pleasure in much right now, even if I'm not actively mad about something. \n\nIt's just hard to care. Kind of like when you have a cold and you can't taste your food, so eating just feels like a chore. I had COVID back in 2021 and I lost my sense of smell and taste for almost a month. It was the weirdest thing. I tried eating a raw onion just to see if I could taste it, and it was just crunchy water. My husband thought I was crazy. He filmed me doing it and put it on Facebook, which I was pretty annoyed about because I was wearing my ratty old bathrobe. It's this ugly pink fleece thing, but it's so warm. I bought it at a thrift store in college for like three dollars. Thrift stores used to be so cheap, but now everyone goes there and they mark everything up. I saw a used candle there once. Half burned! Who buys a half-burned candle? \n\nAnyway, yeah, I guess I just don't really take pleasure in my normal stuff right now. It all just feels a bit pointless."
  }
]
